pub fn bind(documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut bound = bind_unverified(documents.clone());
    crate::repair::verify_bound(&documents, &mut bound.diagnostics);
    bound
}

// Repair checks run the same planner and admission, without recursively
// verifying the diagnostics from a rejected candidate.
pub(crate) fn bind_unverified(mut documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {

                diagnostics.push(crate::parser::repeated_identity(

                    *first_methods,
                    first_site,

    helper_names::normalize(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);

    // Following expansion adds its durable timeline inputs. Recheck before lowering.
    if !feeds.is_empty() { ephemeral::declarations(&documents, &mut diagnostics); }
    let documents = timeline::expand(documents, &mut diagnostics);
    let personas: Vec<String> = documents.iter().flat_map(|d| d.personas.iter().cloned()).collect();
    let mut catalog = Catalog::build(documents, &mut diagnostics);

            "device" | "oblivious" => Err(unsupported(&token.site, &token.value, "pass an explicit bounded argument to the operation")),
            other => Err(syntax(&token.site, format!("unexpected top-level {other:?}"))),

    /// A parameter, local or loop variable: a keyword cannot be one.
    fn local_name(&mut self) -> Fallible<String> {

            let token = self.peek().clone();

    fn parse_statement(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let token = self.peek().clone();
        if token.kind == Kind::Word && self.peek_at(1).value == "=" && self.peek_at(2).value != "=" && reserved::BINDING_WORDS.contains(&token.value.as_str()) {
            return Err(reserved::binding(&token.site, &token.value));
        }
        match token.value.as_str() {
            "while" => return Err(unsupported(&token.site, "while", "use a bounded for domain or walk")),
            "break" => {
                let site = self.take("break")?.site;
                self.newline()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::Break { site });
            }
            "continue" => {
                let site = self.take("continue")?.site;
                self.newline()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::Continue { site });
            }
            "if" => return self.parse_if(),
            "elif" => return Err(unsupported(&token.site, "elif", "else if cond:")),
            "as" => {
                let site = self.take("as")?.site;
                let principal = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
                self.take(":")?;
                self.newline()?;
                let body = self.parse_block()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::As { principal, body, site });
            }
            "for" => return self.parse_for(),
            "require" => return self.parse_require(),
            "return" => {

                if self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) {
                    self.newline()?;
                    return Ok(Stmt::Return { value: Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, site: site.clone() }, site });
                }
                let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;
                if !matches!(value, Expr::Merge(_)) {
                    self.newline()?;
                }
                return Ok(Stmt::Return { value, site });
            }
            "emit" => {
                let site = self.take("emit")?.site;
                let effect = self.word()?.value;
                let payload = self.expression(0, &["once", "<newline>"])?;
                let once = if self.maybe("once") { Some(self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?) } else { None };
                self.newline()?;
                return Ok(Stmt::Emit { effect, payload, once, site });
            }
            "device" | "oblivious" => return Err(unsupported(&token.site, &token.value, "pass an explicit bounded argument to the operation")),
            _ => {}
        }
        if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(1).value == "=" {
            let name = self.peek().clone();
            let binding = self.local_name()?;
            self.take("=")?;
            let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;

            self.take(")")?;
            self.take("=")?;

            self.newline()?;
            return Ok(Stmt::Bind { names, value, site });
        }
        let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;
        if !matches!(value, Expr::Merge(_)) {
            self.newline()?;
        }
        let site = value.site().clone();
        Ok(Stmt::Expr { value, site })
    }

    fn parse_if(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let site = self.take("if")?.site;
        let condition = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
        self.take(":")?;
        self.newline()?;
        let then_body = self.parse_block()?;
        let mut else_body = Vec::new();

            self.take("else")?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.1 — else if is a nested conditional with the same scope and flow rules.
            if self.at("if") {
                else_body.push(self.parse_if()?);
            } else {
                self.take(":")?;
                self.newline()?;
                else_body = self.parse_block()?;
            }
        } else if self.at("elif") {
            return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site, "elif", "else if cond:"));
        }
        Ok(Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, site })
    }

    fn parse_for(&mut self) -> Fallible<Stmt> {
        let site = self.take("for")?.site;
        let name = self.local_name()?;
        self.take("in")?;
        let domain = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
        self.take(":")?;
        self.newline()?;
        let body = self.parse_block()?;
        Ok(Stmt::For { name, domain, body, site })
    }

    fn tuple_binding_ahead(&self) -> bool {

            return false;
        }
        cursor += 1;

//! Reserved words: Quarry keywords cannot name a column or a binding. The
//! refusal names the word at its declaration, before a later use fails.

use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site};

/// Words that begin a table line (`by`, `read <-`, `after insert`, …).
pub(crate) const TABLE_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "after", "by", "cap", "delete", "deliver", "ephemeral", "expire", "immutable", "insert", "online",
    "public", "read", "retain", "search", "sync", "unique", "update",
];

/// Words a statement or expression reads as syntax, so a parameter, local or
/// loop variable of that name cannot be used (or silently means the keyword).
// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — commit/new/now/viewer are contextual builtins, so declarations cannot shadow them.
pub(crate) const BINDING_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    "and", "as", "break", "commit", "continue", "created", "delete", "device", "elif", "else", "emit",
    "exists", "false", "for", "if", "insert", "merge", "new", "not", "now", "null", "oblivious", "or",
    "require", "return", "true", "update", "upsert", "viewer", "walk", "while",
];

fn rename(word: &str) -> String {
    match word {
        "read" => "isRead".into(),
        "by" => "author".into(),
        "after" => "afterAt".into(),
        "update" => "change".into(),
        "delete" => "removal".into(),
        "insert" => "addition".into(),
        "public" => "isPublic".into(),
        "unique" => "isUnique".into(),
        "sync" => "synced".into(),
        "cap" => "capacity".into(),
        "expire" => "expiresAt".into(),
        "not" => "startsAt".into(),
        "online" => "isOnline".into(),
        "search" => "searchText".into(),
        "retain" => "retained".into(),
        "immutable" => "locked".into(),
        "ephemeral" => "transient".into(),
        "deliver" => "delivery".into(),
        "walk" => "outing".into(),
        "viewer" => "who".into(),
        "now" => "at".into(),
        "commit" => "committedAt".into(),
        "new" => "newId".into(),
        other => format!("{other}Value"),
    }
}

pub(crate) fn column(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word: it begins a table line (`{}`), so it cannot name a column", usage(word)),
        format!("rename the column, for example `{name}:`, and use `.{name}` in rules and queries (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn binding(site: &Site, word: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let name = rename(word);
    Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RESERVED",
        format!("`{word}` is a reserved word, so it cannot name a parameter, local or loop variable"),
        format!("rename it, for example `{name}`, at this declaration and at each use (reserved words: snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RESERVED)"))
}

pub(crate) fn repeated_identity(
    site: &Site,
    first: (bool, bool),
    first_site: &Site,
    repeated: (bool, bool),
) -> Diagnostic {
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#9 — identity is project-wide and repeated declarations must name both method sets.
    let methods = |(password, guests)| match (password, guests) {
        (true, true) => "password and guests",
        (true, false) => "password",
        (false, true) => "guests",
        (false, false) => "no methods",
    };
    Diagnostic::new(
        site,
        "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX",
        format!("`use identity` is project-wide: {} already declared at {first_site}; this repeated declaration names {}", methods(first), methods(repeated)),
        "keep one declaration: `use identity` (password and guests), `use identity password`, or `use identity guests`",
    )
    .guide("production")
}

fn usage(word: &str) -> &'static str {
    match word {
        "read" | "insert" | "update" | "delete" => "read <- rule, insert <- rule, update <- rule, delete <- rule",
        "by" | "unique" => "by name: columns, unique columns",
        "after" => "after insert <- exists …",
        "public" => "public 'why'",
        "sync" | "online" => "sync … / online only",
        "cap" => "cap N by column",
        "expire" => "expire at .column",
        "not" => "not before .column",
        "immutable" => "immutable columns",
        "retain" => "retain until-revoked",
        "ephemeral" => "ephemeral 5s",
        "search" => "search column",
        _ => "a table line",
    }
}

live contributions under the read meter; a large group can be unavailable.
The client then asks the server, which uses the backlog-bounded read. Physical
totals and private next-expiry metadata are not delivered to bypass that limit.
Live scalar misses and empty pages remain incomplete when source coverage is
incomplete. An inherited
aggregate over a public source with subset delivery runs on the server;
its local scalar and page coverage is unknown, including for the viewer.

Filtered counts disclose predicate membership, calendar groups disclose bucket
membership, and rank discloses relative values to the aggregate's read audience.
The filter grants no source-row read. N_MAINTAIN_DELTA names dependencies;
restrict the aggregate read rule when the delta is private (guide rules).
When that delta is an intended, reviewed product disclosure, record the review
without changing authority:

```quarry
maintain monthlyCounts: count escorts by zone, month(.at)
  read <- exists staff[viewer]
  disclosure reviewed 'staff intentionally see monthly zone totals'
```

The rationale silences N_MAINTAIN_DELTA for this maintain. It does not make the
source or aggregate public and does not weaken either table's write rules.

Even a hidden aggregate can disclose a constraint outcome: near an integer
endpoint, a hidden selected contribution can make a write fail with
E_OVERFLOW while an excluded contribution commits. Work reservation does not
prevent this overflow bit; the refusal names only the view and column,
and the failed transaction rolls back.

//! Calibration 23: repair expiry-scan admission first; retain the receipt-gap fallback.
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Store, Value, WriteState};
use snapback4_lang::{compile, guide};

fn recipe() -> String {
    guide::quarry(guide::card("sequences").unwrap()).into_iter()
        .find(|s| s.contains("mutation skipExpiredBefore")).unwrap()
}
fn checked(s: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound {
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), s.into())]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(b.notes.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.notes);
    b
}
fn id(s: &str) -> Value { Value::Id(s.into()) }
fn write(e: &mut Engine, op: &str, args: &[(&str, Value)], ids: &[&str], now: i64) -> Value {
    let ctx = Context::new("alice", args.iter().map(|(k,v)| (k.to_string(), v.clone())).collect())
        .with_ids(ids.iter().map(|s| s.to_string())).at(now);
    match e.mutate(op, None, ctx).unwrap().state {
        WriteState::Sent { result, .. } => result,
        state => panic!("{op}: {state:?}"),
    }
}

#[test]
fn diary_and_run_log_intents_reach_the_recipe_directly() {
    for phrase in ["receipt gap expiring messages", "receipt gap", "receipt gaps", "expiring messages",
        "skip expired gaps in receipts", "advance delivered prefix over expired messages",
        "resume receipts after messages expire", "move receipt watermark past expired messages",
        "disappearing message receipt", "Bounded unique ordinal probes", "SEQUENCES",
        "what did this member miss", "bounded unique ordinal probes", "expired ordinal holes",
        "mutation page over expiring messages", "prohibited mutation scan over expiring rows", "sequence by"] {
        assert_eq!(guide::card(phrase), guide::card("sequences"), "{phrase}");
    }
    checked(&recipe());
    assert!(guide::explain_code("E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT").unwrap().contains("sequence by conversationId"));
}

#[test]
fn sequences_card_leads_with_admitted_receipt_reads_and_runtime_backlog_bounds() {
    let card = guide::card("receipt gap expiring messages").unwrap();
    assert!(card.contains("reads the first live blocker before deleting runs or writing"));
    assert!(card.contains("mutation delivered(conversationId: conversations, from: int, through: int): server"));
    assert!(card.find("mutation delivered(").unwrap() < card.find("fn skipExpired_messages").unwrap());
    assert!(card.contains("the ceiling refuses `E_BOUND` atomically"));
    assert!(card.contains("For shapes still refused"));
    assert!(!card.contains("E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT refuses a mutation page over expiring messages"));
}

fn refused_gap(keys: &str) -> String {
    let scan_index = if keys.contains("conversation, ordinal") { "" } else { "  by byConversationOrdinal: conversation, ordinal, id\n" };
    format!("table conversations:\n  public\n  online only\ntable messages:\n  conversation: conversations\n  other: conversations\n  ordinal: int\n  externalNumber: int\n  expiresAt: time ?\n{keys}\n{scan_index}  public\n  expire at .expiresAt\n  online only\neffect observeGap\nmutation gap(conversation: conversations):\n  emit observeGap {{}}\n  return first(messages where .conversation = conversation and .ordinal > 0 first 1 by .ordinal)\n")
}

// @ref LLP 3000.025#3-proposed-rule — these scans now admit before effects; the receipt helper remains an alternative after the phase ends.
fn assert_initial_read_admitted(source: &str) {
    let source = source.replace("  emit observeGap {}\n", "");
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert_eq!(b.schema.operations["gap"].predicts.as_deref(), Some("never"));
}

#[test]
fn receipt_helper_keeps_the_source_boundary_repair_first() {
    let source = refused_gap("  unique conversation, ordinal");
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
    assert_eq!(b.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let d = &b.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(d.code, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
    assert!(d.message.contains("after `emit observeGap`"), "{d}");
    let repair = d.rewrite.find("above the entire `emit observeGap`").expect(&d.rewrite);
    let helper = d.rewrite.find("fn skipExpired_messages").expect(&d.rewrite);
    assert!(repair < helper && d.rewrite.contains("Fallback"), "{d}");
    // Moving the scan ahead of the emit is sufficient; no helper is needed.
    checked(&source.replace("  emit observeGap {}\n  return first(", "  blocker = first(")
        .replace("first 1 by .ordinal)\n", "first 1 by .ordinal)\n  emit observeGap {}\n  return blocker\n"));
}

#[test]
fn receipt_helper_keeps_the_prediction_repair_first() {
    let source = refused_gap("  unique conversation, ordinal")
        .replace("  online only\n", "  sync public last 500 by .id\n")
        .replace("  emit observeGap {}\n", "")
        .replace("mutation gap(", "mutation inspect(");
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
    assert_eq!(b.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let d = &b.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(d.code, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
    let repair = d.rewrite.find("Make `inspect` `: server`").expect(&d.rewrite);
    let helper = d.rewrite.find("fn skipExpired_messages").expect(&d.rewrite);
    assert!(repair < helper && d.rewrite.contains("Fallback"), "{d}");
    assert!(!d.rewrite.contains("Move this scan"), "no effect ends this prefix: {d}");
    let repaired = checked(&source.replace("mutation inspect(conversation: conversations):", "mutation inspect(conversation: conversations): server"));
    assert_eq!(repaired.schema.operations["inspect"].predicts.as_deref(), Some("never"));
}

#[test]
fn receipt_helper_keeps_both_prediction_and_source_boundary_repairs() {
    let source = refused_gap("  unique conversation, ordinal")
        .replace("  online only\n", "  sync public last 500 by .id\n");
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
    assert_eq!(b.diagnostics.len(), 1, "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let d = &b.diagnostics[0];
    assert_eq!(d.code, "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT");
    let prediction = d.rewrite.find("Make `gap` `: server`").expect(&d.rewrite);
    let boundary = d.rewrite.find("above the entire `emit observeGap`").expect(&d.rewrite);
    let helper = d.rewrite.find("fn skipExpired_messages").expect(&d.rewrite);
    assert!(prediction < boundary && boundary < helper, "{d}");
    checked(&source.replace("mutation gap(conversation: conversations):", "mutation gap(conversation: conversations): server")
        .replace("  emit observeGap {}\n  return first(", "  blocker = first(")
        .replace("first 1 by .ordinal)\n", "first 1 by .ordinal)\n  emit observeGap {}\n  return blocker\n"));
}

fn helper_follows_the_scan_coordinate_regardless_of_key_order_or_other_indexes() {
        } else {
            let helper = catalog.functions.get(name)?;
            Some(match &helper.body {
                FunctionBody::Block(body) => body.clone(),
                FunctionBody::Expression(value) => vec![Stmt::Return { value: value.clone(), site: value.site().clone() }],
            })

    }
    fn definitions(catalog: &Catalog, expr: &Expr) -> Vec<(String, bool)> {
        match expr {
            Expr::Call { name, .. } if catalog.functions.contains_key(name) => vec![(name.clone(), false)],
            Expr::ShapeUse { name, .. } => vec![(name.clone(), true)],
            Expr::Name { name, .. } if catalog.shapes.contains_key(name) => vec![(name.clone(), true)],
            Expr::Shape { fields, .. } => fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::NamedSpread { name, .. } = field { Some((name.clone(), true)) } else { None }).collect(),
            _ => vec![],
        }
    }
    fn effects(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> (bool, bool) {
        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements);
        let mut writes = false;
        let mut binds = false;
        fn bindings(body: &[Stmt]) -> bool {
            body.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt {
                Stmt::Bind { .. } => true,
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => bindings(then_body) || bindings(else_body),
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => bindings(body),
                _ => false,
            })
        }

                if let Some(body) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                    let (w, b) = effects(catalog, &body, visiting); writes |= w; binds |= b;

    fn effectful(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {

    fn ordered_call(catalog: &Catalog, value: &Expr) -> bool {
        matches!(value, Expr::Call { name, args, .. } if catalog.functions.contains_key(name) && args.iter().all(|arg| !effectful(catalog, arg)))

    fn call_facts(catalog: &Catalog, name: &str, args: &[Expr], known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        let mut out: BTreeSet<_> = known.intersection(global).cloned().collect();
        let Some(helper) = catalog.functions.get(name) else { return out };
        let readonly = helper_body(catalog, name, false).is_some_and(|body| !effects(catalog, &body, &mut BTreeSet::new()).0);
        // Use fresh intermediate names so caller variables that happen to share
        // a formal's spelling cannot be mistaken for transferred evidence.
        for fact in known {
            let source = format!("query guardFact():\n  require {fact} else GUARD\n  return null\n");
            let (doc, errors) = crate::parser::parse_document("guard.q", &source);
            if !errors.is_empty() { continue; }
            let Some(Stmt::Require { condition, .. }) = doc.programs.first().and_then(|p| p.body.first()) else { continue };
            let mut condition = condition.clone();
            let mut used = BTreeSet::new();
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { used.insert(name.clone()); } });
            used.extend(helper.args.iter().map(|param| param.name.clone()));
            for arg in args {
                let mut copy = analysis_expr(arg);
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr { used.insert(name.clone()); } });
            }
            let mut substitutions = Vec::new();
            for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
                if is_viewer(arg) || (!readonly && !matches!(arg, Expr::Name { .. })) { continue; }
                let mut marker = "diagSnapshot".to_owned();
                while used.contains(&marker) { marker.push('_'); }
                used.insert(marker.clone());
                substitutions.push((fact_text(arg), marker, param.name.clone()));
            }
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                let text = fact_text(expr);
                if let Some((_, marker, _)) = substitutions.iter().find(|(arg, _, _)| *arg == text) {
                    *expr = Expr::Name { name: marker.clone(), site: expr.site().clone() };
                }
            });
            let mut valid = true;
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr && name != "viewer" && !substitutions.iter().any(|(_, marker, _)| marker == name) { valid = false; }
                if matches!(expr, Expr::Focal { .. }) { valid = false; }
            });
            if valid {
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut condition, &mut |expr| {
                    if let Expr::Name { name, .. } = expr && let Some((_, _, param)) = substitutions.iter().find(|(_, marker, _)| marker == name) { *name = param.clone(); }
                });
                out.insert(fact_text(&condition));
            }
        }
        for (param, arg) in helper.args.iter().zip(args) {
            if is_viewer(arg) { out.insert(format!("({} = viewer)", param.name)); }
        }
        out
    }
    fn rebound_names(statements: &[Stmt], out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Bind { names, .. } => out.extend(names.iter().cloned()),
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { rebound_names(then_body, out); rebound_names(else_body, out); }
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => rebound_names(body, out),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {

    }

    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {

    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,

    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };

                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
            }
        }
        (value, ty)
    }

//! Rule evaluation and the static facts about it that the engine hoists: a
//! rule that is proven true by the scanned prefix (`decided_true`) costs no
//! probes; a rule whose deciding fields are pinned by the prefix (`uniform`) is
//! decided once for the whole range. Copied from Snapback 2's uniformity
//! analysis (LLP 2000.000 §5.1); the certificate that priced these is gone,
//! the analysis is not.

use crate::ir::{Bound, Expr, IndexKeyExpr, Input, Merge, Scan};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema, Table};
use crate::value::Value;
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Visibility {
    /// One verdict serves every row of the scanned prefix.
    pub uniform: bool,
    /// That verdict is `true` before any row is read.
    pub decided_true: bool,
}

pub fn scan_visibility(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Visibility {
    let Some(table) = schema.table(&scan.table) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let Some(index) = table.index(&scan.index) else { return Visibility::default() };
    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).find_map(|(part, key)| (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then(|| key.scalar()).flatten());
    visibility(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned)

}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.5 — a missing upsert may insert only when the complete held group and its key decide that no hidden target can exist.
pub fn upsert_absence(schema: &Schema, name: &str, index: &str, key: &[Expr]) -> Result<Visibility, &'static str> {
    let table = schema.table(name).ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    let sync = table.sync.as_ref().ok_or("the target is online only")?;
    if sync.horizon.is_some() { return Err("the target has a horizon"); }
    let rule = table.rules.read.device_audience_rule();
    // Public subset sync has no whole-table absence proof. Its group coverage
    // is a separate decision; do not infer it merely from the public read rule.
    if rule.is_uniformly_readable() && sync.audience != crate::schema::Audience::Public { return Err("the key's synced group is not established"); }
    let index = table.index(index).ok_or("the key does not pin readability")?;
    let pinned = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(key).filter_map(|(part, expression)| part.column().map(|column| (column, expression))));
    let visibility = prefix_visibility(name, table, &rule, &pinned);
    if !visibility.uniform { return Err("the key does not pin readability"); }
    Ok(visibility)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — simplify self-witnesses, then pre-check only the leading conjuncts whose fields the scope pins, in evaluation order.
pub(super) fn prefix_decision(schema: &Schema, scan: &Scan) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    let table = schema.table(&scan.table)?;
    let index = table.index(&scan.index)?;
    let pinned: BTreeMap<_,_> = crate::small_map::collect(index.components.iter().zip(&scan.prefix).filter_map(|(part, key)| part.column().zip(key.scalar())));
    if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| pinned.contains_key(&t.reference)) { return Some(table.rules.read.clone()); }
    let rule = simplify_prefix(&table.rules.read, &scan.table, table, &pinned);
    if matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Allow) { return None; }
    let mut terms = Vec::new();
    conjuncts(rule, &mut terms);
    let decided: Vec<_> = terms.into_iter().take_while(|term| {
        let mut fields = BTreeSet::new();
        term.fields(&mut fields);
        fields.iter().all(|field| pinned.contains_key(field))
    }).collect();
    (!decided.is_empty()).then_some(RuleExpr::And(decided))
}

// Flatten only conjunctions, preserving left-to-right short-circuit order.
fn conjuncts(rule: RuleExpr, out: &mut Vec<RuleExpr>) {
    match rule { RuleExpr::And(terms) => for term in terms { conjuncts(term, out); }, rule => out.push(rule) }
}

fn simplify_prefix(rule: &RuleExpr, name: &str, table: &Table, pinned: &BTreeMap<&String, &Expr>) -> RuleExpr {
    let pin = |name: &str| pinned.iter().find_map(|(key, value)| (key.as_str() == name).then_some(*value));
    if rule_known(rule, true, name, table, &pin) { return RuleExpr::Allow; }
    if rule_known(rule, false, name, table, &pin) { return RuleExpr::Deny; }
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) => {
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            for term in terms { conjuncts(simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned), &mut out); }
            out.retain(|term| !matches!(term, RuleExpr::Allow));
            RuleExpr::And(out)
        },
        RuleExpr::Or(_) => {
            // Use the same self-witness absorption as uniformity, but remove
            // the absorbed field read from the expression we actually run.
            let terms = rule.disjuncts();
            let mut out: Vec<_> = terms.iter()
                .filter(|term| !terms.iter().any(|other| self_probe_absorbs(name, table, term, other)))
                .map(|term| simplify_prefix(term, name, table, pinned))
                .filter(|term| !matches!(term, RuleExpr::Deny))
                .collect();
            match out.len() {
                0 => RuleExpr::Deny,
                1 => out.pop().unwrap(),
                _ => RuleExpr::Or(out),
            }
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => {
            let inner = simplify_prefix(inner, name, table, pinned);
            negate_required_bool(&inner, table).unwrap_or_else(|| RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(inner)))
        }
        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => required_bool_equality(left, right, table, false).unwrap_or_else(|| rule.clone()),
        _ => rule.clone(),
    }
}

/// Required booleans have exactly two values, so their negated equality and
/// inequality forms can share one positive equality identity. Optional bools
/// retain their original expression because `null` is distinct from `false`.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#9 — prefix simplification and guard coverage use the same required-Boolean identity.
fn required_bool_equality(left: &RuleTerm, right: &RuleTerm, table: &Table, equal: bool) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    [(left, right), (right, left)].into_iter().find_map(|(field, value)| {
        let (RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Bool(expected))) = (field, value) else { return None };
        matches!(table.columns.get(column), Some(ColumnType::Bool)).then(|| RuleExpr::Eq(
            Self::Eq(left, right) | Self::Ne(left, right) | Self::Compare(_, left, right) => {
                term(left, out);
                term(right, out);
            }
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| item.fields(out)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.fields(out),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
                key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).for_each(|item| term(item, out));
            }
        }
    }

    /// The disjuncts of the rule: an `or` flattens, anything else is itself.
    pub fn disjuncts(&self) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
        match self {
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().flat_map(|item| item.disjuncts()).collect(),
            other => vec![other],
        }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.6 — service seats read on the server and never enlarge a device's audience.
    pub fn service_grant(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)))
            | Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(principal)), RuleTerm::Viewer)
                if principal.strip_prefix("service:").is_some_and(|name| !name.is_empty()) => Some(principal),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    /// Remove only entire service disjuncts; a conjunct, negation or ordinary
    /// principal equality must still pass the ordinary exact-audience check.
    pub fn device_audience_rule(&self) -> std::borrow::Cow<'_, RuleExpr> {
        let terms = self.disjuncts();
        if !terms.iter().any(|term| term.service_grant().is_some()) { return std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(self); }

    fn input_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, value: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        value_nodes(value, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes));
    }
    fn expression_guards(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, value: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        if observe_prefix {
            super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes));
        } else {
            // Ordinary query values share the input-value boundary: point and
            // prefix probes in row-dependent candidate filters are visible hits.
            value_nodes(&copy, &mut |expr| expression_guard_node(catalog, schema, program, expr, known, global, system, false, visiting, notes));
        }
    }
    fn invalidate(names: &[String], known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        known.retain(|fact| !fact.split(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() && c != '_').any(|word| names.iter().any(|name| name == word)));
    }

    /// An expression in an identity position: a text literal becomes an id.
    pub(super) fn expr_as_identity(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if let A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. } = expr {
            if text.is_empty() {
                return Err(type_error(expr.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
            }
            return Ok((Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())), expected.required()));
        }
        self.expr(expr, out)
    }

    }

    fn parse_focal_rule_value(&mut self, label: &str, site: &Site) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        if self.maybe("<-") {
            // `read <- public 'reason'`: the table line's spelling, allowed on
            // the rule too; the reason rides as the call's one argument.
            if self.peek().value == "public" && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Str {
                let site = self.take("public")?.site;
                let reason = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
                return Ok(Expr::Call { name: "public".into(), args: vec![Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(reason.value), site: reason.site }], site });
            }
            let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;
            current_rule(&value)?;
            return Ok(value);
        }
        let binding = self.word()?.value;
        self.take("<-")?;
        let value = self.expression(0, &["<newline>"])?;
        Err(syntax_with(site, format!("{label} rules use the focal row . and do not take a named single-row binding"), format!("{label} <- {}", print::expression(&focalize(&value, &binding)))))
    }

    fn parse_shape_declaration(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        let site = self.take("shape")?.site;
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        if self.at("from") {
            return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site.clone(), "imports", format!("every .q file beside schema.q shares one namespace: declare `shape {name}` once, in any file, and use it anywhere")));
        }
        if self.at("on") && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Word && self.peek_at(2).value == "=" {
            let at = self.peek().site.clone();
            return Err(syntax_with(&at, "shapes infer their row type; omit on table", "=").mechanical());
        }
        self.take("=")?;
        self.take("{")?;
        let fields = self.parse_shape_fields()?;
        self.take("}")?;
        self.newline()?;
        self.document.shapes.push(ShapeDecl { name, fields, site });
        Ok(())
    }

    fn parse_function_declaration(&mut self) -> Fallible<()> {
        let site = self.take("fn")?.site;

                statements.push(Stmt::Return { value: Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, site: site.clone() }, site: site.clone() });
            }
            FunctionBody::Block(statements)
        } else {

//! Expressions: precedence climbing over Quarry's operators, the postfix
//! forms (reads, ranges, shapes, presence assertions), merges, writes, walks
//! and the refusals for forms Snapback 4 does not have.

use super::*;

fn optional_filter_equalities(value: &Expr) -> Option<Vec<crate::diag::Site>> {
    let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = value else { return None; };
    if op != "or" { return None; }
    for (guard, predicate) in [(left, right), (right, left)] {
        let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site } = guard.as_ref() else { continue; };
        if op != "=" { continue; }
        let name = match (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) {
            (Expr::Name { name, .. }, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. }) |
            (Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Null, .. }, Expr::Name { name, .. }) => name,
            _ => continue,
        };
        let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, site: predicate_site } = predicate.as_ref() else { continue; };
        if !matches!(op.as_str(), "=" | "in" | "<" | "<=" | ">" | ">=") { continue; }
        let column = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Member { base, .. } if matches!(base.as_ref(), Expr::Focal { .. }));
        if (column(left) && matches!(right.as_ref(), Expr::Name { name: value, .. } if value == name))
            || (column(right) && matches!(left.as_ref(), Expr::Name { name: value, .. } if value == name)) {
            return Some(vec![site.clone(), predicate_site.clone()]);
        }
    }
    None
}

impl Parser {
    pub(super) fn expression(&mut self, minimum: u8, stops: &[&str]) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let start = self.position;
        let mut left = self.prefix(stops)?;
        loop {
            let token = self.peek().clone();
            if self.is_stop(&token, stops) {
                break;
            }
            let Some(precedence) = precedence(&token.value) else { break };
            if precedence < minimum {
                break;
            }
            self.expression_spans.insert((left.site().line, left.site().col), start..self.position);
            self.position += 1;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.3 — recover through guessed equality without admitting an alias or cascading through its block.
            let adjacent_equals = |next: &Token, col| next.kind == Kind::Symbol && next.value == "=" && next.site.line == token.site.line && next.site.col == col;
            if token.kind == Kind::Symbol && token.value == "=" && adjacent_equals(self.peek(), token.site.col + 1)
                && !adjacent_equals(self.peek_at(1), token.site.col + 2) {
                self.diagnostics.push(syntax_with(&token.site, "equality is = in Quarry", "=").mechanical());
                self.position += 1;
            }
            let right = self.expression(precedence + u8::from(token.value != "??"), stops)?;
            if precedence == 4 {
                self.comparison_spans.insert((token.site.line, token.site.col), start..self.position);
            }
            left = Expr::Binary { op: token.value, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right), site: token.site };
            // Optional-filter guards admit the SQL/host-language equality
            // spellings locally; unrelated guessed == retains its repair.
            if let Some(sites) = optional_filter_equalities(&left) {
                self.diagnostics.retain(|d| !(d.message == "equality is = in Quarry" && sites.contains(&d.site)));
            }
        }
        if minimum == 0 && self.at("?") {
            let site = self.take("?")?.site;
            let yes = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let no = self.expression(0, stops)?;
            return Err(unsupported(&site, "? :", format!("{} if {} else {}", print::expression(&yes), print::expression(&left), print::expression(&no))));
        }
        // `a if cond else b`, below every operator: the value is one of the
        // two, and the other is never evaluated.
        if minimum == 0 && self.at("if") && !stops.contains(&"if") && self.peek_at(1).value != "absent" {
            let site = self.take("if")?.site;
            let mut inner: Vec<&str> = stops.to_vec();
            inner.push("else");
            let condition = self.expression(0, &inner)?;
            if !self.maybe("else") {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a conditional value takes both arms: value if condition else other", format!("{} if {} else null", print::expression(&left), print::expression(&condition))));
            }
            let otherwise = self.expression(0, stops)?;
            left = Expr::If { then: Box::new(left), condition: Box::new(condition), otherwise: Box::new(otherwise), site };
        }
        self.expression_spans.insert((left.site().line, left.site().col), start..self.position);
        self.document.source.record(&left, &self.source, &self.tokens[start..self.position]);
        Ok(left)
    }

    pub(super) fn prefix(&mut self, stops: &[&str]) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let start = self.position;
        let token = self.peek().clone();
        if token.kind == Kind::Str {
            self.position += 1;
            return self.postfix(Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(token.value), site: token.site }, stops, start);
        }
        if token.value == "not" {
            self.position += 1;
            let value = self.expression(7, stops)?;
            return self.postfix(Expr::Unary { op: "not".into(), value: Box::new(value), site: token.site }, stops, start);
        }
        if token.value == "-" && !(self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Word && self.peek_at(2).value == "[") {
            self.position += 1;
            let value = self.expression(7, stops)?;
            return self.postfix(Expr::Unary { op: "-".into(), value: Box::new(value), site: token.site }, stops, start);
        }
        if token.value == "exists" || token.value == "created" {
            self.position += 1;
            let mut probe_stops = stops.to_vec();
            probe_stops.push("<probe-shape>");
            let value = self.prefix(&probe_stops)?;
            let value = if token.value == "exists" && let Expr::Name { name, site } = &value {
                Expr::Read(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, order_keys: Vec::new(), computed: Default::default(), table: name.clone(), keys: Vec::new(), index: Some("byId".into()), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() })
            } else { value };
            let Expr::Read(read) = value else {
                return Err(syntax(value.site(), format!("{} requires an indexed read t[key]", token.value)));
            };
            if read.range.is_some() || (token.value == "created" && (!read.seeks.is_empty() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some())) {
                let plain = ReadExpr { range: None, matching: if token.value == "exists" { read.matching.clone() } else { None }, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), ..read.clone() };
                let message = if token.value == "exists" { "exists takes a domain read; omit first, last and cursor clauses" } else { "created takes a plain indexed read; no where, first, last, after or seek" };
                return Err(syntax_with(&read.site, message, format!("{} {}", token.value, print::read_expr(&plain))));
            }
            return self.postfix(if token.value == "exists" { Expr::Exists { read, site: token.site } } else { Expr::Created { read, site: token.site } }, stops, start);
        }
        if token.value == "walk" {
            return self.parse_walk();
        }
        if matches!(token.value.as_str(), "insert" | "update" | "upsert" | "delete") {
            return self.parse_write();
        }
        if (token.value == "+" && (self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Word || self.peek_at(1).value == "~")) || token.value == "~" || (token.value == "-" && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Word && self.peek_at(2).value == "[") {
            return Err(syntax_with(&token.site, "write sigils are invalid; use insert, update, upsert, or delete", "use insert, update, upsert, or delete"));
        }
        if token.value == "merge" {
            return self.parse_merge().map(Expr::Merge);
        }
        if token.value == "device" || token.value == "oblivious" {
            return Err(unsupported(&token.site, &token.value, "pass an explicit bounded argument to the operation"));
        }
        let value = match token.kind {
            Kind::Str => {
                self.position += 1;
                Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Str(token.value.clone()), site: token.site }
            }
            Kind::Number => {
                self.position += 1;
                let mut value = token.value.clone();
                if self.peek().site.line == token.site.line && self.peek().site.col == token.site.col + token.value.len() as u32
                    && let Some(multiplier) = super::views::duration_unit(&self.peek().value) {
                    self.position += 1;
                    value = token.value.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(multiplier))
                        .filter(|n| *n <= snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX)
                        .ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&token.site, "duration exceeds the representable integer range", "use a smaller integer duration, such as 24h"))?.to_string();

                } else { "unique positional key selected by argument types" });
                normalized.to_mut().index = Some(candidates[0].name.clone());
            }
        }
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        let point = mode != ReadMode::Range;
        self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
        let named = keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| c.is_some()) && !keys.is_empty();
        let mut selection = if read.index.is_some() { "explicit by" } else if named { "named equality columns" } else { "compatible positional key types" };
        // A `words(..)` index serves one read: `matching` with exactly its
        // leading columns as keys. Every other read sees plain indexes only.
        let searching = mode == ReadMode::Range && read.matching.is_some();
        let mut candidates: Vec<&ast::IndexDecl> = info
            .indexes
            .iter()
            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|explicit| explicit == &index.name))
            .filter(|index| read.seeks.iter().all(|b| index.columns.get(keys.len()) == Some(&b.column)))
            .filter(|index| if searching { index.searches_after(keys.len()) } else { index.words.is_none() })
            .filter(|index| match mode {

            })
            .filter(|index| {

                    if index.columns.len() < keys.len() { index.columns.iter().all(|column| keys.iter().any(|(c, _, _)| c.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| index.columns[..keys.len()].contains(c.as_ref().unwrap())) }
//! Admission of replica-independent unique point keys. Populations and
//! maintained null groups have their own semantics.
use super::catalog::TableInfo;
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, Diagnostic};

// Arguments remain in authored order, with the binder's normalized column
// names and presence facts (including control-flow narrowing).
pub(super) fn check(table: &TableInfo, index: &IndexDecl, read: &ReadExpr, arguments: &[(Option<&str>, bool)], rule: bool) -> Result<(), Diagnostic> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — the complete maintained group is unique even with nulls.
    // Extra predicates (such as value = 1) may use a secondary access index.
    // A partial group or a value-only key must still pass ordinary admission.
    if table.maintained.is_some() && table.indexes.iter().any(|key| key.name == "byGroup"
        && key.columns.iter().all(|column| index.columns.contains(column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column))) {
        return Ok(());
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — a fully pinned superset of a required unique key is itself unique.
    // Preserve role-bearing rule/postcondition indexes without adding a
    // redundant uniqueness constraint or changing their physical selection.
    let unique = index.unique || table.indexes.iter().any(|key| table.total_unique(key)
        && key.columns.iter().all(|column| index.columns.contains(column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column)));
    if !unique {
        let repair = if rule {
            "declare a non-null unique key for this probe; table rules cannot scan a population, so move population work into a query or mutation before storing the fact the rule tests"
        } else {
            "use a bounded population read (`where … first N`) or declare a non-null unique key"
        };
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POINT_KEY",
            format!("{}.{} is not unique: a point probe can choose different tied rows across replicas, even with non-null arguments", read.table, index.name),
            repair).guide("indexes"));
    }
    if !index.unique || table.maintained.is_some() { return Ok(()); }
    let named = arguments.iter().all(|(column, _)| column.is_some());
    let mut guards = Vec::new();
    let mut nulls = Vec::new();
    for (position, (column, present)) in arguments.iter().enumerate() {
        let column = if named { column.unwrap() } else { index.columns[position].as_str() };
        if index.columns.iter().any(|key| key == column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column)
            && table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| ty.optional) && !present {
            if matches!(&read.keys[position].value, crate::ast::Expr::Literal { value: crate::ast::Literal::Null, .. }) { nulls.push(column); }
            else { guards.push(column); }
        }
    }
    if guards.is_empty() && nulls.is_empty() { return Ok(()); }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — null ties have no replica-independent point;
    // require proof of presence without changing total population equality.
    let mut advice = Vec::new();
    if !nulls.is_empty() { advice.push(format!("replace literal null with a non-null value for {}", nulls.join(", "))); }
    if !guards.is_empty() {
        advice.push(format!("prove non-null arguments for {} with required types or presence guards", guards.join(", ")));
    }
    // Recovery choices are design advice. Do not print a source edit without
    // a context proof (a population can need a different index or bound).
    Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_NULL_KEY",
        format!("{}.{} is a nullable unique key: a null argument can tie several rows; a point is ambiguous across replicas because each device may hold different tied rows. {}; alternatively read a bounded population or key the row by id (guide indexes)", read.table, index.name, advice.join("; ")),
        "").guide("indexes"))
}

//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {

totals and private next-expiry metadata are not delivered to bypass that limit.
Live scalar misses and empty pages remain incomplete when source coverage is
incomplete. An inherited
aggregate over a public source with subset delivery runs on the server;
its local scalar and page coverage is unknown, including for the viewer.

Filtered counts disclose predicate membership, calendar groups disclose bucket
membership, and rank discloses relative values to the aggregate's read audience.
The filter grants no source-row read. N_MAINTAIN_DELTA names dependencies;
restrict the aggregate read rule when the delta is private (guide rules).
When that delta is an intended, reviewed product disclosure, record the review
without changing authority:

```quarry
maintain monthlyCounts: count escorts by zone, month(.at)
  read <- exists staff[viewer]
  disclosure reviewed 'staff intentionally see monthly zone totals'
```

The rationale silences N_MAINTAIN_DELTA for this maintain. It does not make the
source or aggregate public and does not weaken either table's write rules.

Even a hidden aggregate can disclose a constraint outcome: near an integer
endpoint, a hidden selected contribution can make a write fail with
E_OVERFLOW while an excluded contribution commits. Work reservation does not
prevent this overflow bit; the refusal names only the view and column,
and the failed transaction rolls back.

Adding or changing a filter, key, duration, offset, dependent literal default or index
is a derived rebuild at its declaration. Today's atomic activation rebuilds
populated stores before publishing the new generation; it is not resumable.
Attribution capture runs inside the same atomic activation, before that rebuild, preserving existing paths.

    assert!(!d.rewrite.contains("skipExpired"), "{d}");

#[test]
fn uniform_membership_rule_still_requires_the_callers_authority_check() {
    let source = refused_gap("  unique conversation, ordinal")
        .replace("table messages:", "table members:\n  conversation: conversations\n  principal: principal\n  unique conversation, principal\n  public\n  online only\ntable messages:")
        .replace("  public\n  expire", "  read <- exists members[.conversation, viewer]\n  expire");
    assert_initial_read_admitted(&source);
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.clone())]);
    let d = b.diagnostics.iter().find(|d| d.code == "E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT").unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("{:?}", b.diagnostics));
    assert!(d.rewrite.contains("skipExpired"), "uniformity alone is not caller authority");
    assert!(d.message.contains("prefix justifies advancing a receipt only after the caller's own authority check"), "{d}");
    assert!(guide::card("sequences").unwrap().contains("prefix justifies advancing a receipt only after the caller's own authority check"));
    // The safe integration explicitly checks membership. No membership means
    // refusal, whether a live hidden row occupies the gap or it is absent.
    let schema = source.split("mutation gap").next().unwrap();
    let b = checked(&format!("{schema}\n{}\nmutation gap(conversation: conversations):\n  require exists members[conversation, viewer] else NOT_MEMBER\n  return skipExpired_messages(conversation, 0, 3)\n", d.rewrite));
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    for occupied in [false, true] {
        if occupied {
            e.seed_at([("messages".into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
                ("id", id("hidden")), ("conversation", id("chat")), ("other", id("other")),
                ("ordinal", Value::Int(1)), ("externalNumber", Value::Int(100)), ("expiresAt", Value::Null),
            ]))], 1).unwrap();
//! The catalog: every table (declared or generated for a maintain) with its
//! columns and indexes, plus the named shapes and functions, and where each
//! schema path was declared so a core validation issue can point at a line.

use crate::ast::*;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Site, name_rewrite, unknown};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct TableInfo {
    pub through: Option<snapback4_core::schema::through::Through>,
    pub captures: BTreeMap<String, ScalarType>,
    pub recorded: BTreeMap<String, ScalarType>,
    pub decl: TableDecl,
    pub columns: BTreeMap<String, ScalarType>,
    pub indexes: Vec<IndexDecl>,
    /// Set when the table is a maintain's generated target.
    pub maintained: Option<MaintainDecl>,
}

impl TableInfo {
    pub fn total_unique(&self, index: &IndexDecl) -> bool {
        index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.iter().all(|column| column == "id" || self.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.optional))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.1 — a scan cursor carries declared components only;
    // uniqueness with a nullable component cannot resolve ties between nulls.
    pub fn total_cursor(&self, index: &IndexDecl) -> bool {
        index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id") || index.unique && index.words.is_none()
            && index.columns.iter().all(|column| match index.computed.get(column) {
                Some(expr) => super::time_indexes::key(&self.decl.name, &self.columns, column, expr).is_ok(),
                None => self.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| !ty.optional),
            })
    }
}

#[derive(Default)]
pub struct Catalog {
    pub ordinary_searches: Vec<String>,
    pub source: crate::source::SourceMap,
    pub feed_views: BTreeMap<String, super::feed_view::Plan>,
    pub extremum_domains: BTreeMap<String, ReadExpr>,
    pub read_origins: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub canonical_read_origins: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub view_reads: BTreeMap<String, crate::ast::MergeExpr>,
    pub view_types: BTreeMap<String, crate::types::Ty>,
    pub planner_notes: Vec<Diagnostic>,
    /// Exact cap declarations that would admit an otherwise fully verified
    /// owner-or-membership page, keyed by the read's source site.
    pub membership_cap_repairs: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub synthesized: std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>,
    pub documents: Vec<Document>,
    pub tables: BTreeMap<String, TableInfo>,
    pub maintains: BTreeMap<String, MaintainDecl>,
    pub shapes: BTreeMap<String, ShapeDecl>,
    pub functions: BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl>,
    pub effects: BTreeMap<String, String>,
}

impl Catalog {
    pub fn build(documents: Vec<Document>, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> Self {
        let mut tables: BTreeMap<String, TableInfo> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut maintains = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut shapes: BTreeMap<String, ShapeDecl> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut functions: BTreeMap<String, FunctionDecl> = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut effects = BTreeMap::new();
        let mut effect_sites: BTreeMap<String, Site> = BTreeMap::new();
        for doc in &documents {
            for table in &doc.tables {
                if tables.contains_key(&table.name) {
                    diagnostics.push(unknown(&table.site, format!("duplicate table {}", table.name), "rename one of the two tables"));
                    continue;
                }
                let mut columns = BTreeMap::new();
                let mut ok = true;
                for column in &table.columns {
                    if column.name == "id" {
                        diagnostics.push(unknown(&column.site, format!("table {} gets id implicitly; remove the id column", table.name), "delete this line"));
                        ok = false;
                        continue;
                    }
                    if columns.insert(column.name.clone(), column.type_.clone()).is_some() {
                        diagnostics.push(unknown(&column.site, format!("duplicate column {}.{}", table.name, column.name), "rename one of the two columns"));
                        ok = false;
                    }
                }
                for field in &table.search {
                    if !matches!(columns.get(field), Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Text { .. } | TypeKind::Enum { .. }, .. })) {
                        diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&table.site, format!("search {} requires a text or enum column on {}", field, table.name), format!("declare `{field}: text <=2000` or `{field}: enum('one', 'two')` before `search {field}`")).guide("search"));
                    }
                }
                let mut indexes = vec![IndexDecl { name: "byId".into(), columns: vec!["id".into()], unique: true, refusal: None, words: None, computed: BTreeMap::new(), site: table.site.clone() }];
                for index in &table.indexes {
                    if indexes.iter().any(|existing| existing.name == index.name) {
                        diagnostics.push(unknown(&index.site, format!("duplicate index {}.{}", table.name, index.name), "name one ordering explicitly"));
                        ok = false;
                        continue;
                    }
                    for column in &index.columns {
                        if index.computed.contains_key(column) { continue; }
                        if column != "id" && !columns.contains_key(column) {
                            diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&index.site, format!("index {}.{} names unknown column {column}", table.name, index.name), name_rewrite(column, columns.keys().map(String::as_str).chain(["id"]), None)));
                            ok = false;
                        }
                    }
                    for field in index.words.iter().flat_map(|(_, fields)| fields) {
                        match columns.get(field) {
                            Some(ScalarType { kind: TypeKind::Text { .. } | TypeKind::Enum { .. }, .. }) => {}
                            // The table stays in the catalog: one line is
                            // wrong, and its readers should hear about that
                            // line, not about a table that vanished.
                            Some(other) => diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&index.site, format!("index {}.{} tokenizes {field}, which is {}, not text or enum", table.name, index.name, crate::print::scalar_type(other)), format!("words(<a text or enum column of {}>)", table.name))),
                            None => diagnostics.push(crate::diag::type_error(&index.site, format!("index {}.{} tokenizes unknown column {field}", table.name, index.name), name_rewrite(field, columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None))),
                        }
                    }
                    for (name, expr) in &index.computed {
                        if let Err(error) = super::time_indexes::key(&table.name, &columns, name, expr) { diagnostics.push(error); }
                    }
                    indexes.push(index.clone());
                }
                let mut seen_effects = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
                for rule in &table.rules {
                    if !seen_effects.insert(rule.effect) {
                        if rule.effect == Effect::Read && table.rules.iter().any(|r| matches!(&r.value,Expr::Call{name,..} if name=="$readThrough")) {

                        } else { diagnostics.push(unknown(&rule.site, format!("duplicate {} rule on {}", rule.effect.word(), table.name), "keep one rule per effect")); }
                        ok = false;
                    }
                }
                if ok {

    pub(super) fn parse_read(&mut self, table: &str, site: &Site) -> Fallible<ReadExpr> {
        self.take("[")?;
        let mut keys = Vec::new();
        let mut seeks = Vec::new();
        while !self.at("]") {
            let key_site = self.peek().site.clone();
            let mut column = None;
            let mut end = 1;
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) {
                while self.peek_at(end).value == "." && self.peek_at(end + 1).kind == Kind::Word { end += 2; }
            }
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(end).value == "!=" {
                return Err(syntax_with(&key_site,
                    "probe brackets take named equalities, not != tests",
                    "pin a full unique key and compare the row field outside the brackets, or use a bounded program `exists table[key] where .column != value`; program probes see only caller-readable rows, while table-rule probes see all rows; use unique/cap/after insert constraints for invariants across hidden rows"));
            }
            if self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && self.peek_at(end).value == "=" {
                column = Some(self.maintain_column()?);
                self.take("=")?;
            }
            if column.is_none() && self.at_kind(Kind::Word) && matches!(self.peek_at(end).value.as_str(), ">" | ">=" | "<" | "<=") {
                let column = self.maintain_column()?;
                let op = self.peek().value.clone();
                self.position += 1;
                let value = self.expression(0, &[",", "]"])?;
                seeks.push(SeekBound { column, op, value, site: key_site });
            } else {
                if !seeks.is_empty() { return Err(syntax_with(&key_site, "pin the prefix before the seek comparisons", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index")); }
                let value = self.expression(0, &[",", "]"])?;
                keys.push(KeyPart { column, value, site: key_site });
            }
            if !self.maybe(",") {
                break;
            }
        }
        self.take("]")?;
        Ok(ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: table.to_owned(), keys, index: None, range: None, filter: None, matching: None, seeks, site: site.clone() })
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_range(&mut self, mut read: ReadExpr, start: usize, probe: bool) -> Fallible<ReadExpr> {

    fn write_key(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, keys: &[ast::KeyPart], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<WriteKey> {
        if keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} needs a key: which row?", info.decl.name), format!("{}[id]", info.decl.name)));
        }
        // A row value as the key addresses the row by its id.
        if keys.len() == 1 && keys[0].column.is_none() {
            let mut row_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.expr(&keys[0].value, &mut row_setup)?;
            if let Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) = t.required()
                && table == info.decl.name
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }
        let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: info.decl.name.clone(), keys: keys.to_vec(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() };
        let (bound, index, _) = self.select_index(info, &read, ReadMode::Point, out)?;
        super::point_key::check(info, &index, &read, &bound.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, bound)?;
        if index.name == "byId" {
            let id = self.temp("key");
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: key[0].clone() });
            return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
        }
        Ok(WriteKey { index: index.name, key, alternate: true })
    }

    fn write_row(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, fields: &[ObjectField], current: Option<&str>, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        objects::explicit_fields(fields.iter().filter(|f| !f.spread).map(|f| (f.name.as_str(), &f.site)))?;
        let expanded;
        let fields = if fields.iter().any(|field| field.spread) {
            expanded = self.expand_object_fields(fields, Some(info), out)?;
            &expanded
        } else { fields };
        for field in fields {
            if super::columns::fields(info).any(|name|name == field.name) {
                return Err(super::columns::refusal(&field.site,format!("{}.{} is engine-maintained; write its source rows instead",info.decl.name,field.name)));
            }
use snapback4_lang::{compile, diag::SourceRewriteKind, Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, value::object};

fn refusal(source: &str, code: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    bound.diagnostics.iter().find(|diagnostic| diagnostic.code == code).cloned()
        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("missing {code}; diagnostics: {:#?}", bound.diagnostics))
}

fn emitted_source_compiles(source: &str, authored: &str, diagnostic: &Diagnostic) -> String {
    let emitted = diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_ref().expect("a verified source rewrite");
    let repaired = source.replacen(authored, emitted, 1);
    assert_ne!(repaired, source, "fixture must contain the authored source");
    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired.clone())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "emitted rewrite did not compile:\n{emitted}\n{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    repaired
}

fn classified(diagnostic: &Diagnostic, expected: SourceRewriteKind) {
    let actual = diagnostic.source_rewrite_kind.as_ref().expect("a concrete rewrite category");
    // This deliberately stays exhaustive: a new concrete rule-edit category
    // makes the golden fail to compile until it has a fixture and semantics case.
    match actual {
        SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey),
        SourceRewriteKind::MembershipBootstrap => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::MembershipBootstrap),
        SourceRewriteKind::WriteResultNarrowing => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::WriteResultNarrowing),
        SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate => assert_eq!(expected, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate),
    }
}

#[test]
fn every_concrete_rule_rewrite_is_the_emitted_text_and_compiles_in_its_schema() {
    let positional = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('staff', 'member')\n  unique principal, role\n  public 'roles'\ntable records:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'staff']\n  online only\nquery visible():\n  return records first 10 by .id\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(positional, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey);
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(), Some("roles[principal = viewer, role = 'staff']"));
    emitted_source_compiles(positional, "roles[viewer, role = 'staff']", &diagnostic);

    let update = "table fees:\n  status: enum('pending', 'confirmed')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed'\n  online only\nmutation transition(fee: fees, status: enum('pending', 'confirmed')):\n  update fees[fee] { status }\n  return null\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(update, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(), Some("update (old, next) <- (old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed')"));
    emitted_source_compiles(update, "update <- old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed'", &diagnostic);

    let partially_named = "table fees:\n  owner: principal\n  status: enum('pending', 'archived')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- .owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived'\n  online only\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(partially_named, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(
        diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(),
        Some("update (old, next) <- (next.owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived')")
    );
    let repaired = emitted_source_compiles(
        partially_named,
        "update <- .owner = viewer and old.status != 'archived'",
        &diagnostic,
    );
    assert!(repaired.contains("update (old, next)") && repaired.contains("next.owner"));

    let literal_field_text = "table fees:\n  owner: principal\n  label: text <=40\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.label = '.owner' and .owner = viewer\n  online only\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(literal_field_text, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(
        diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(),
        Some("update (old, next) <- (old.label = '.owner' and next.owner = viewer)")
    );
    emitted_source_compiles(
        literal_field_text,
        "update <- old.label = '.owner' and .owner = viewer",
        &diagnostic,
    );

    let underscore_field = "table fees:\n  _owner: principal\n  status: enum('pending', 'archived')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.status != 'archived' and ._owner = viewer\n  online only\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(underscore_field, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    classified(&diagnostic, SourceRewriteKind::NamedUpdate);
    assert_eq!(
        diagnostic.source_rewrite.as_deref(),
        Some("update (old, next) <- (old.status != 'archived' and next._owner = viewer)")
    );
    emitted_source_compiles(
        underscore_field,
        "update <- old.status != 'archived' and ._owner = viewer",
        &diagnostic,
    );
}

#[test]
fn emitted_source_rewrites_match_independent_reference_semantics() {
    let positional = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('staff', 'member')\n  unique principal, role\n  public 'roles'\ntable records:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'staff']\n  online only\nquery visible():\n  return records first 10 by .id\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(positional, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN");
    let repaired = emitted_source_compiles(positional, "roles[viewer, role = 'staff']", &diagnostic);
    // Execute the actual compiler-emitted replacement over the independent
    // population-membership truth table.
    for has_pair in [false, true] {
        let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired.clone())]);
        let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap();
        let mut rows = vec![("records".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("record".into())), ("owner", Value::Id("owner".into()))]))];
        if has_pair {
            rows.push(("roles".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("role".into())), ("principal", Value::Id("alice".into())), ("role", Value::String("staff".into()))])));
        }
        engine.seed(rows).unwrap();
        let read = engine.query("visible", Context::new("alice", Default::default())).unwrap();
        let Value::Array(rows) = read.data else { panic!("expected page, got {:?}", read.data) };
        assert_eq!(!rows.is_empty(), has_pair);
    }

    let update = "table fees:\n  status: enum('pending', 'confirmed')\n  public 'fees'\n  update <- old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed'\n  online only\nmutation transition(fee: fees, status: enum('pending', 'confirmed')):\n  update fees[fee] { status }\n  return null\n";
    let diagnostic = refusal(update, "E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR");
    let repaired = emitted_source_compiles(update, "update <- old.status = 'pending' and next.status = 'confirmed'", &diagnostic);
    // Execute the actual named-update line for every old/next status pair.
    for old in ["pending", "confirmed"] {
        for next in ["pending", "confirmed"] {
            let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired.clone())]);
            let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap();
            engine.seed([("fees".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("fee".into())), ("status", Value::String(old.into()))]))]).unwrap();
            let outcome = engine.mutate("transition", None, Context::new("alice", [
                ("fee".into(), Value::Id("fee".into())), ("status".into(), Value::String(next.into())),
            ].into())).unwrap();
            let allowed = matches!(outcome.state, WriteState::Sent { .. });
            let old_pending = old == "pending";
            let next_confirmed = next == "confirmed";
            assert_eq!(allowed, old_pending && next_confirmed, "{old} -> {next}: {outcome:?}");
        }
    }

    let parents = "table scenes:\n  revision: int\n  public 'scenes'\ntable boundaries:\n  revision: int\n  public 'boundaries'\ntable instructions:\n  scene: scenes\n  boundary: boundaries\n  revision: int\n  read <- .boundary.revision = .scene.revision\n  online only\nquery visible():\n  return instructions first 10 by .id\n";

    let predicate = diagnostic.rewrite.split('`').skip(1).step_by(2)

    let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired.clone())]);
    assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "emitted stored-field predicate did not compile:\n{predicate}\n{:#?}", bound.diagnostics);
    // Execute the actual emitted predicate under the invariant named by the

            let bound = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired.clone())]);
            let mut engine = Engine::memory(bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|program| program.program)).unwrap();
            engine.seed([
                ("scenes".into(), object([("id", Value::Id("scene".into())), ("revision", Value::Int(scene))])),
