//! Seeks and row-local scan predicates.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.011.000.001#4 — retain the binder's actual choice for why, never infer a new lookup or rewrite.
    pub(super) fn record_selection(&mut self, site: &str, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, result: &str, reason: &str) {
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let bindings = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(position, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(position) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let origin = if self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())) { "synthesized; " } else { "" };
        let source = if origin.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; read: {}", self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(read))) };
        let mut selection = format!("selected {} ({bindings}); result: {result}; {origin}{reason}{source}", index.name);
        if !self.variants.is_empty() {
            let when = self.variants.iter().map(|(name, present)| format!("{} {}", name.trim_start_matches("$intentPresent:").trim_start_matches("$intentSearch:"), if *present { "present" } else { "absent" })).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
            selection = format!("when {when}: {selection}");
        }
        self.selections.entry(site.into()).and_modify(|prior| { if !prior.contains(&selection) { prior.push_str("; "); prior.push_str(&selection); } }).or_insert(selection);
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#10 — a covered total unique key reads one row; additional named equalities check that row.
    pub(super) fn read_value(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        for key in &read.keys { if let Some(field) = &key.column { super::super::row_views::predicate_field(info, field, &read.site)?; } }
        if !read.seeks.is_empty() { return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek needs a bounded page", "t[prefix, col >= start] first 50 by index")); }
        let mut point = read.clone();
        if point.keys.is_empty() && info.maintained.as_ref().is_some_and(|maintain| maintain.by.is_empty()) {
            point.keys.push(ast::KeyPart { column: None, value: A::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(true), site: read.site.clone() }, site: read.site.clone() });
            point.index = Some("byGroup".into());
        } else if point.keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(&read.site, format!("{}[] reads nothing; a point read needs a key", read.table), format!("{}[id]", read.table)));
        }
        let implicit_group = mode == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.maintained.is_some() && point.index.is_none();
        if implicit_group { point.index = Some("byGroup".into()); }
        let (mut keys, index, mut selection) = self.select_index(info, &point, mode, out)?;
        super::super::point_key::check(info, &index, read, &keys.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        if implicit_group { selection = "complete maintained group key".into(); }

    /// `connected[p]`: how many connections the principal holds open on the
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Shapes

Shapes choose the fields returned from a row or page.
Name a shape to reuse the same result layout across reads.

```quarry
query nestedRoster(c: cursor ?):
  return members where .principal = viewer first 20 by .roomId after c { roomId, roster: members[roomId = .roomId], room: rooms[.roomId] { name } }
```

The outer page selects your memberships.
The named inner key binds that membership's room before reading its roster.
`room` expands the reference into an object, or null when missing or hidden.
Inside each `where`, `.` denotes that read's own row.

## Limits

- Comparing a filtered row column with itself is refused, except optional-column `<=` and `>=` presence tests: E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY.
- Spread a present row or object with known fields: E_QUARRY_SPREAD.
- Write each explicit field once: E_QUARRY_DUPLICATE_FIELD.
- Values joined by branches must have compatible row kinds: E_QUARRY_SHAPE_JOIN.
- Move or narrow a shape that cannot fit the meter: E_SHAPE_EXCEEDS_METER.

## Details

Shapes choose the fields returned from a row or page.
Name a shape to reuse the same result layout across reads.

```quarry
shape brief = { id, body, author: .authorId }                        -- a named shape: bare columns, and `.` for the row

query shapeForms(roomId: rooms, messageId: messages, c: cursor ?):
  page = messages where .roomId = roomId after c last 20 by .at { *, room: rooms[.roomId], count: messageCounts[.roomId] }   -- over a page: `*` spreads every column; a point read inside is null for a hidden row
  row = rooms[roomId] { id, name, tagCount: count(.tags) }           -- over a row: an object; over a nullable row: null when the row is
  named = messages[messageId] brief                                  -- a named shape by its name
  nested = rooms[roomId] { ...card, extra: 1 }                        -- `...name` spreads a named shape's fields
  return { page, row, named, nested }
```

```quarry
table listings:
  title: text
  city: text
  public
table bookings:
  guest: principal
  listing: listings
  start: time
  read <- .guest = viewer
query myBookings(c: cursor ?):
  return bookings where .guest = viewer last 20 by .start after c { *, listing: listings[.listing] { title, city } }
query listingChoice(key: listings, flag: bool):
  p = listings[key] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return ({ ...p } if flag else { ...(p { * }) }) { title: 'override', * }
```

Here `listing` is the expanded object, or null for a missing or hidden listing.

In the first example, table and shape names are the language card's. A shape over a page is
a bounded loop; its fields are the row's columns by bare name, `*` for all
of them, `...shape` for a named shape's fields, and `name: expr` with `.`
as the row. There is no `select` spelling.
A table-typed helper accepts that table's row or reference. A reference's
field reads its row: `.slot.day` is `slots[.slot].day`, null when missing or
hidden; `slots[.slot] ! else NOT_FOUND` refuses instead. Shapes are projections,
not declared helper-argument types.
A row-based shape keeps `pending?: boolean` from its base, including `*`, named
spreads and subsequent shapes. It is this device's unconfirmed change, absent
after confirmation; it is not a schema column or metadata on a containing record.

## Shapes and helpers

`guide reads` owns paging and continuation; `guide programs` owns helpers.
"##;

                    }
                    let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };

VERDICT: NEEDS_REVISION

- [bind/program.rs:574](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:574): Binding positional operands before index selection bypasses identity-literal validation. With only the implicit `byId` key, `return exists items['']` previously reached `expr_as_identity` and refused the empty id. It now binds ordinary text, then converts it directly to `Value::Id("")` at line 704. The extracted contextualization restores row projection but not this validation. Preserve empty-id refusal without reevaluating operands, and add positional-versus-explicit-index regression coverage.

- [parser/expr.rs:344](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/expr.rs:344): The unconditional trailing-`desc` refusal intercepts valid named-shape applications. For example, declaring `shape desc = { id }` and returning `logs last 20 by .at desc` previously applied that shape; this amendment rejects it and recommends removing the projection. Preserve declared shape resolution, including cross-file declarations, while diagnosing unresolved SQL-style `desc`.

- [bind/positional_keys.rs:58](/tmp/w12-diag-review-r4/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/positional_keys.rs:58): The foreign-table lead still assumes the intended key is the target’s id. For `rolls` declaring `secret: secrets`, `number: int`, and `unique secret, number`, `rolls[user, 1]` with `user: users` correctly identifies an incompatible foreign operand, but incorrectly advises passing a `rolls` reference. That replacement still cannot satisfy the composite key; the required reference is `secrets`. Derive expected types from the relevant key positions, or retain general key advice after the actual-foreign-table lead. Add this incompatible-first-operand composite regression alongside the compatible-first-operand counterexample.
